#7452. Google Says Target Frequency Can Mix Non-Skippable Ads & Shorts via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Target frequency campaigns can pair non-skippable ads with Shorts and in-feed ads, an updated help page says. Here's what changed and where the limits are.

Google has revised its help page on Target frequency campaigns, which says advertisers may now use non-skippable in-stream ads with Shorts and in-feed ads within the same campaign.

On this page, advertisers are allowed to “mix any combination of skippable in-stream, non-skippable in-stream (up to 20s), bumper, In-feed, and Shorts ads” in a campaign.

There is no date referenced on the page. However, the capture by the Internet Archive from Oct. 14 still shows the earlier rule: “Currently, non-skippable ads can’t be combined with In-feed or Shorts ads in Target Frequency campaigns.” Prior to the update, advertisers who were using the Target frequency option had to choose whether to use non-skippable ads or the Shorts and in-feed ad placements options.

Campaigns limited to in-stream formats can’t add in-feed or Shorts, which Google’s page warns “may result in less reach or a higher cost-per-thousand impressions (CPM)” for those campaigns.

In the Oct. 14 version of the Target frequency page, selecting non-skippable ads would “automatically disable In-feed and Shorts ads” in the campaign. The current setup steps say to select the Non-skippable in-stream checkbox to include those ads “alongside your selected formats” in a multi-format campaign.

The page’s “up to 20s” label for non-skippable ads wasn’t in the Oct. 14 version. On Google’s Video reach campaigns page, the recommended non-skippable length for Target frequency went from 7 to 15 seconds in the Oct. 14 capture to 7 to 20 seconds now. That page’s Target frequency table now also lists in-feed and Shorts, and recommends a vertical video of 6 to 60 seconds next to horizontal 15- and 6-second videos.

The current page lists a single frequency range of 2 to 7 per week or 4 to 12 per month. The Oct. 14 capture gave that range for multi-format campaigns and 2 to 4 per week (4 to 8 per month) for in-stream-only campaigns. When I covered Target frequency’s global launch in 2022, Google said advertisers could pick a goal of up to four per week.

The same Target frequency campaign may use both non-skippable video ads and either Shorts ads or in-feed ads; however, it will need to be limited to auction buying, as the help page makes reserve buying available on “non-mixed formats” only.

While legacy campaigns which are in-stream-only cannot change from one ad format type to another once they are created (a rule also on the page before the update), multi-format campaigns do allow changing the ad format types in campaign settings. At present (Nov. 12), the page does not provide information about what happens when adding non-skippable ads to a multi-format campaign created before the update to see if you can still include in-feed and Shorts ads without dropping them.

The Target frequency campaign help page FAQ also explains that an existing campaign cannot be converted into a Target frequency campaign and instructs the advertiser to “end your current campaign and set up a new Target frequency campaign” in its place.

At the time I looked at the English Target frequency page on Nov. 12, it showed the new mixing rule. However, both the UK English and Australian English versions of the Target frequency page were stating you cannot mix in-feed or Shorts ad placements and non-skippable video ad placements. The UK English version of the setup guide continues to list a separate weekly range of 2 to 4 for in-stream formats.

In addition, Display & Video 360’s own Target frequency help page which provides assistance for those purchasing using this channel shows that non-skippable video ads are available “up to 15 seconds” only when purchased as an in-stream-only format.

Google Ads Help’s New features & announcements section had no announcement regarding the update as of Nov. 12. The last announcement published there was dated May 20.

Per Google’s updated help page, brands buying Target frequency at auction no longer have to choose between non-skippable ads and Shorts or in-feed reach.

Prior to developing a campaign for Target frequency using auctions, you should create a new Target frequency campaign, open the Ad formats drop-down within that campaign and verify if selecting Non-skippable in-stream leaves both in-feed and Shorts selected within your account.

The Video reach page says that horizontal video can serve on Shorts (when there is no vertical video in the ad group) and eligible horizontal videos may be served vertically when video enhancements are turned on.

Google suggests creating vertical video content for Shorts and horizontal video content for feed ads and non-skippable ads.

Watch for the UK and Australian versions of the Target frequency page to match the English page, and for any change to Display & Video 360’s page. Also watch the English page for a note on adding non-skippable ads to campaigns created before the update.

#7449. Meta publishes explainer on Your Algorithm controls

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The feature enables users to opt in or out of certain topics and customize their feeds based on personal preferences.

Meta has shared some new insights into how its Your Algorithm control option on Facebook works, which enables users to provide direct input into what content they want to see more or less of in the app.

The tool enables users to add or remove specific topics from an automatically generated list of interests, which Facebook's system has built based on each user's in-app actions. In addition to removing and adding topics, users can also now tell the algorithm specifically what they want to see more of, via a recently added chatbot prompt.

Conceptually, this gives users more ways to refine their Facebook experience.

On the Meta engineering blog, the company has shared notes on how the process works, and explained more about how exactly updating user algorithm preferences can impact in-app experiences.

In addition to these topic-related notes, Meta said the system also used demotion rules to enact more general parameters that don’t relate to a specific topic.

“Quality-related requests, such as reducing AI-generated or sexually suggestive content, map to dedicated demotion signals rather than topic matching,” Meta said.

These demotion rules work differently than topic ranking, with the system using these as indicators to reduce these elements.

“During ranking, the same embedding-based comparison used in the relevance check removes candidates too close to an interest in Not Interested In, and demotion rules push down near matches,” Meta said.

In other words, this feature can reduce the presence of artificial intelligence-generated content in a user’s Facebook feed, though it won’t get rid of that entirely, and ranking will still be influenced by future engagement.

When a user requests less content from a specific creator, however, “that creator’s content is filtered out directly,” Meta said.

These notes provide more insight into how Meta plans to facilitate algorithm customization and give users more control over their in-app experiences.

Meta’s hope is that through tools like this, users will feel more empowered in the app. In turn, that may quell ongoing calls for the platform to return to a reverse chronological feed and remove ranking elements entirely.

Given that algorithmic amplification has continued to drive up Facebook and Instagram usage, Meta is very keen to keep that in place.

Though the company may not have a choice. Last month, the Australian government began preliminary investigations into potential new laws that would force social media platforms to provide users with an option to switch off algorithmic recommendations and set non-algorithmic feeds as the default.

A growing body of research suggests that algorithmic amplification can have harmful impacts. If this new push in Australia goes ahead and it ends up showing that the negative impacts of algorithms are reduced, that could lead to more regions exploring similar rules.

As noted, Meta is very keen to avoid this outcome.

Last year on a Q2 earnings call with analysts, Meta CEO Mark Zukerberga said AI recommendations had led to a 5% increase in time spent on Facebook, and a 6% boost on Instagram for the quarter.

On its Q2 earnings call a year later, Zuckerberg said that global time spent on Instagram had grown in the quarter by double digits year over year, and Facebook video time spent had increased by 9% globally, "largely driven by improvements to our feed and Reels recommendations."

It makes sense, then, that Meta is keen to avoid any external push to switch off its algorithmic ranking.

As such, Meta's hoping that the introduction of this control option will ease user concerns. Combine this with the fact that, based on past history, users rarely update their settings, and the company's likely hoping that it can benefit from the perception of control afforded by the tool, while also seeing a minor impact on its business.

#7446. AI Bot Management: Why SEOs Need More Than Block Lists via @sejournal, @chrisgreenseo

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Do you know which bots your CDN is blocking? Robots.txt is voluntary, and real enforcement may be happening without your SEO team knowing.

For years, bot management in SEO was relatively straightforward. You either allowed crawlers to access your website or blocked them. That model was never perfect, but it was generally sufficient for what we needed.

AI search has made that approach increasingly inadequate.

Today, websites are accessed by traditional search crawlers, AI training crawlers, retrieval bots, user-initiated fetches, and autonomous agents. These serve very different purposes, yet we regularly lump them together under the term “AI bots.”

Blocking a crawler used for model training has different consequences from blocking one retrieving content for search answers – this distinction really matters!

Robots.txt remains useful, but as it’s voluntary actual enforcement happens through CDNs, web application firewalls and bot mitigation services, which can contradict your robots.txt rules without the SEO team even knowing.

Agents complicate matters further. They can browse websites like real users, trigger analytics tracking, and become increasingly difficult to distinguish from human visitors. Block an agent performing a product comparison, and you might not simply lose a ranking. You could disappear from the answer entirely.

My argument is that we need to move from block lists to deliberate bot policies. Policies that consider who’s accessing our content, why they’re doing it and the consequences of allowing or blocking them.

These decisions increasingly involve SEO, engineering, security, legal, and commercial teams. They need to be coordinated, consistently enforced and regularly reviewed.

I’ve covered these challenges, their implications, and some practical approaches in my GEO Elite presentation. Hope you find it useful:

This post was originally published on AI Search Marketing with Chris Green.

#7444. X experiments with expanded AI transparency labels

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The company is testing an automated way to detect and tag posts that may have been generated using artificial intelligence.

X is developing new AI transparency labels for posts, which will not only indicate whether AI was used to generate an update, but also how it was applied in each instance.

Michael O’Herlihy, X’s head of brand safety, shared some examples of these new labels during a presentation at the Brand & AI Safety Summit in New York on Tuesday.

As shown on these example screens, posted by Adweek senior tech reporter Kendra Barnett on X, X’s new AI content labels will include more detailed information on why a post was flagged for AI content. This will better explain the rationale X’s automated enforcement system is using, while also giving creators more opportunities to refute these labels by offering them a more detailed understanding of any potential concerns.

The hope is that these expanded notes will help X refine its AI detection process by prompting more creators to provide feedback on the specifics. This information can then be used to train the system.

At the same time, the tags will add more transparency for users, who may be less likely to re-share a post that’s been flagged for AI content.

O’Herlihy added that X increased its reliance on automated tagging of AI violations because it’s much faster. The automation should also ensure that more harmful content is refuted in-stream.

It could be a good way to enhance transparency in the AI era. However, false positives could also be problematic for creators, who could get annoyed if their content is unfairly tagged.

#7440. Elon Musk’s Grok AI faces legal challenges over nudification

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Multiple plaintiffs allege that the chatbot is directly responsible for creating naked images based on photos taken of them as children, per Bloomberg.

Elon Musk’s xAI is facing questions regarding who is ultimately responsible for the generation of harmful content via generative artificial intelligence tools. At the heart of these issues is whether the fault lies with the person who tells the system to create this material, or the platform that facilitates this functionality.

These problems lie at the core of multiple legal cases involving xAI’s Grok chatbot, with plaintiffs in three U.S. states seeking legal recourse against xAI over the creation of CSAM material.

As reported by Bloomberg, xAI has come under investigation in three high-profile cases.

The company has denied responsibility in these cases, and said these depictions were generated by users, not by xAI itself.

Musk, xAI’s owner, has also made similar public statements.

In January, amid reports that Grok was being used to generate thousands of nudified images every day, Musk posted: “Anyone using Grok to make illegal content will suffer the same consequences as if they upload illegal content.”

However, shortly after making that announcement, and facing a potential ban of X apps from app stores, xAI changed its policies and usage restrictions on Grok to ensure that it could no longer be used to generate nudified images at scale.

The change in approach showed that xAI had the capacity to restrict this functionality whenever it wanted. However, the company instead initially refused to restrict Grok usage on free speech grounds, and settled on user autonomy as its legal defense.

Indeed, xAI has even challenged new laws designed to outlaw the generation of non-consensual naked images.

As reported by Reuters last month, xAI succeeded in blocking the implementation of Minnesota’s proposed ban on AI-generated naked images by arguing that the law was unconstitutional and infringed on free speech rights. The company was the only provider actively opposing this law.

At the same time, xAI has repeatedly touted Grok’s systematic capacity to generate what is called “spicy” content or offensive material in its “unhinged mode.” The company has also launched companion chatbots that can engage in NSFW chats.

As such, this does seem like more of a feature than a bug, yet xAI is hoping it can hang onto the fact that it’s not liable for usage that goes beyond certain stated parameters.

Establishing legal responsibility could set a new precedent in AI image generation, and it will be interesting to see whether any of these cases results in xAI being held responsible.

#7437. LinkedIn tests frequent viewer feature

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Premium users already see who views their profile, but a new report from Wired said the platform may also soon show how often someone stops by.

LinkedIn users may soon be able to derive more value from its “Who viewed your profile” list, with the platform working on a new feature that would not only show who visited their profile, but also how often each specific user revisited their page within a given period.

That would add more context to LinkedIn’s profile viewer data, although for most users, this is fairly useless information. However, some marketers have highlighted this as a lead-generation opportunity, providing a list of potential warm contacts, based on the fact that they’ve checked out a user’s profile.

A frequency indicator on the same display would enhance that capacity, by showing people who have returned to a user’s profile in the app again and again, which could be an indicator of even stronger interest.

So long as that interest is in a professional capacity.

A visit count for each profile listed could also show users who could be interested in them for other reasons, which could make this a somewhat unsettling display.

LinkedIn users can lock down their profile to connections only, so there would be ways to address this concern, and of course users are under no obligation to contact people who’ve visited their profile.

Wired said that LinkedIn confirmed the feature is currently in testing, but it had no further info to share at this stage.

#7435. Netflix embraces 35mm film in international campaign

“Find the Thing You Never Knew You Needed” consists of three new spots shot in different aspect ratios and a social series.

As U.S. consumers continue to cut back on subscriptions, including for streaming services, Netflix’s latest campaign emphasizes the quality of its vast content library in order to help it stand out in a crowded field. “Find the Thing You Never Knew You Needed” comes as company executives continue to put an emphasis on value and engagement following price hikes and lower projected ad revenue.

Created by Netflix’s in-house shop the Brand Creative Studio and agency Mother, the campaign features three spots shot on 35mm film using different aspect ratios. In “Axolotl,” which is shot for widescreen, an axolotl leverages its quadrupedalism to escape its tank and turn the TV back on after its owners leave in the middle of a “Bridgerton” marathon. “Dawn” follows a vampire who braves the rapidly rising sun to find a working TV in order to watch “Love Is Blind.” In “Hypersleep,” an astronaut who is the only one awake while his fellow shipmates are in cryosleep, wakes someone up in order to show them the “Soda Pop” scene from “KPop Demon Hunters.”

The spots were directed by Vania Heymann and Gal Muggia of the production company Iconoclast. The nature of the ads allows for the featured program to be swapped out depending on the market. In Brazil, the show featured in “Dawn” is crime drama “Os Donos Do Jogo,” or “Rulers of Fortune” in English.

Over the course of four episodes, the social series “Typecast” will feature four creatives and showcase their somewhat unexpected Netflix fixations. The series kicks off with fashion designer Willy Chavarria and his show of choice “Love on the Spectrum.” Other guests include musician Teezo Touchdown, makeup artist Isamaya Ffrench and Randy Savvy of The Compton Cowboys. The videos were filmed in 16mm to fit the feel of the overall campaign.

The campaign’s use of analog film is part of a recent trend that has seen advertisers looking to run counter to the rise of artificial intelligence. Stop-motion animation has been of particular interest, with brands such as Levi’s and Prada embracing the artform. Netflix’s spots, which feature custom-built sets, follow a similar vein of appealing to the desire for human-made advertising.

Netflix grew revenue 12% year over year to $12.9 billion in Q3, per an earnings release. The company lowered its expected ad revenue for the year from $3 billion to $2.7 billion, a drop it attributed to a slower-than-expected rollout of some advertising capabilities.

#7432. Thought leadership remains highly valuable for B2B buyers

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

A new survey report from Edelman and LinkedIn found that brands value professional insight and experience from other professionals.

Edelman has published a new report which examines the impact of thought leadership content on LinkedIn, and how professionals are utilizing thought leader content within their decision-making process.

Based on insights from “more than 1,100 management-level professionals,” Edelman’s 2026 B2B Thought Leadership Impact Report provides notes on the shifting B2B landscape. In particular, it looks at the role professional expertise plays in the artificial intelligence era.

According to the report, that role is still significant.

Edelman’s data showed that 82% of buyers believe that thought leadership is becoming more valuable, with original research, insight and experience now being more of a differentiator.

The data also showed that 89% of buyers view high-quality thought leadership content “makes a company feel like a long-term partner worth choosing.”

What’s more, a brand that doesn’t share thought leadership content can risk being eliminated from consideration for some potential partners.

And while AI tools are now playing a bigger role in the discovery process, the data also showed that most buyers still validate AI recommendations by referring to a brand’s content.

Also worth noting: Buyers are more likely to share thought leadership content in private channels.

There are also some interesting notes on the value of external influencers when it comes to endorsing a business, and the importance of brands continuing to produce thought leadership content to reinforce existing partnerships.

In summary, while product research is changing, and more business buyers are using AI tools to find relevant products, original brand content still plays a key role in winning deals and assuring buyers that they’re making the right decision.

The key differentiation, then, is expertise and unique insight that nobody else can provide. Even in the age of AI, where digital systems can summarize information,  AI can’t provide real, hands-on experience and insights. It also can’t summarize information that doesn’t already exist.

Hence the need for more thought leadership-type content, and for brands to differentiate themselves through expanded sharing of their professional expertise.

#7430. Carnival gamifies cruise experience via interactive Twitch livestream

Streamer iJustine will take on a series of challenges voted on by viewers as she explores a digital Carnival Celebration ship.

Carnival is turning to Twitch to give consumers a unique look at what a trip aboard one of its vessels would entail at a time when the demographics of the average cruise passenger are in flux. The activation comes as the company reported record-high bookings for 2027 and a net income of $1.9 billion in the third quarter, an all-time high for the company. However, gross margin yields fell 1.3%  due to high fueling costs, per an earnings release.

Cruising has become more popular than ever, with a record 22.4 million Americans expected to take a cruise in 2027, up 3.6% year over year, per AAA research. The pre-pandemic total for American cruise travelers was 14.2 million. While the vacation choice is stereotypically associated with older demographics, the average age of an American cruiser is 45.8, and millennials and Gen Z travelers are increasingly drawn to the travel method due to lower costs and value-oriented packages, according to AAA research.

The livestream will feature a custom-built game in which iJustine, who has nearly 55,000 followers on Twitch at press time, will tackle challenges on the simulated Carnival Celebration. The ship’s spaces and activities, as well as destinations from the brand’s Paradise Destinations collection, will be part of the gameplay. Along with input from the audience, Weigand will help keep energy high by introducing challenges, passing on requests from viewers and offering community instructions. iJustine, who will be playing live onboard, will work to earn her cruise director badge and a Fun Meter will track energy levels.The game is over once the meter hits zero.

To encourage participation, audience members will have the chance to win a cruise vacation. The full livestream will be posted to Instagram and TikTok the day after the event so fans can catch up on what they missed. The game will not be made available to the public after the stream.

“We’re constantly looking for new ways to push the boundaries on how we connect with consumers, and Play Carnival takes that thinking to a whole new level,” said Michael Sotelo, vice president of brand, product and social media at Carnival Cruise Line, in press materials.

The effort falls under the “Find Your Fun Again” campaign, which encourages consumers to ditch the stress and monotony of everyday life and find joy aboard a Carnival ship. Actor Nick Offerman took center stage in a spot in which he goes on vacation after smashing his phone due to receiving an overload of notifications.

Amazon-owned platform Twitch has become a go-to for brands looking to experiment with connecting with young consumers. Last year, E.l.f. Cosmetics became the first brand to test Twitch’s livestream shopping ads.

#7427. Pinterest adds holiday shopping tools

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The platform updated its chatbot with trending gift guides and introduced product comparison tools and wish lists.

Pinterest has added some new shopping features for the holidays, including trending gift guides within its Pinterest Assistant chatbot, product comparison tools and wishlists.

First, Pinterest added festive season activations to its Pinterest Assistant chatbot, including full-screen overviews of seasonal concepts based on trends.

As shown in this example screen, the new holiday experience in Pinterest Assistant offers dedicated trending content overviews for gift inspiration, seasonal decorating ideas and more.

Each highlighted product connects to a shoppable Pin, which could provide additional guidance for some of the most common app use cases during the season.

Pinterest also added a compare feature, which provides Pinterest Assistant users with a range of products to consider based on their queries.

The process gives users a carousel-like display of options to choose from, with reviews as well as information on price and availability.

Pinterest said that Compare is available through Pinterest Assistant and boards on mobile, and through the Close-up button for desktop users in the U.S.

Pinterest also added Wishlists, where shoppers can list their ideal product Pins, and get notified about price drops or restocks.

As per Pinterest: “People can create a wishlist to share with family or use it to keep track of gifts for everyone on their list.”

Pinterest said that Wishlists are currently available in a limited iOS test across the U.S. and Canada.

The platform is also rolling out Deal Boards, which will highlight personalized lists of trending deals based on past engagement history. Pinterest will also include a range of curated deals from creators throughout the season.

Finally, Pinterest shared a list of some of the top gift trends, based on increased in-app search activity, that it’s seen thus far this holiday shopping season.

Pinterest has repeatedly highlighted its increasing value as a product discovery and shopping channel. As such, the holiday season is a key time for the app to showcase that value.

These new features are designed to help more people get seasonal inspiration in the app, and helpful alerts on price drops and stock updates could also provide value.

#7426. Maybelline updates ‘Maybe’ tagline as beauty perceptions evolve

Miley Cyrus is leading a new global brand platform promoting changes meant to be more inclusive and empowering for women.

Maybelline is reworking one of its signature brand assets as it tries to adapt to an evolving landscape for beauty and cosmetics. The L'Oréal-owned marketer’s “Maybe she’s born with it” tagline became a fixture of its advertising beginning in 1991, but consumers in 2026 are applying makeup for different reasons.

The company has attempted to modernize “Maybe” in the past. It was at one point dropped for the largely forgotten “Make It Happen” but eventually brought back. Maybelline revamped the “Maybe” tagline for the TikTok era two years ago. The latest overhaul appears more substantial, changing the underlying meaning of the copy, and is described in the release as permanent.

Over half of surveyed global respondents today see beauty as a way to build confidence compared to 26% who see it as a means of looking more attractive to other people or appearing younger for longer, per McKinsey findings cited by Maybelline. The suggestion is that makeup can be something less superficial and more empowering and inclusive.

Tweaks to the marketer’s tagline try to recognize these trends by adjusting the phrasing to sound less like a question being asked about a single woman’s look. Instead, the new “Maybe” is positioned as a declaration of the possibilities all women can achieve by leveraging makeup as a form of self-expression.

“With 'Maybe.', we're building on our legacy and inviting women everywhere, at every stage of life, to explore the possibilities within them,” said Maybelline Global Brand President Sandrine Jolly in a statement.

Maybelline enlisted Cyrus, an ambassador who’s previously voiced the “Maybe” tagline, to lead its new “The Beauty of Infinite Possibility” platform. Cyrus was selected for the effort because her own career has entailed a constantly shifting identity, moving from child star to hard-partying club song purveyor and then back to her country roots.

Cyrus released her latest album, “Bass Persuades,” in September, which could draw additional interest to the Maybelline partnership. “Bass Persuades” sees Cyrus again dabbling in the dance pop and electronic music genres.

Products featured in the campaign include Maybelline’s Serum Lipstick, Lash Sensational Sky High Mascara and Instant Eraser Concealer. Maybelline will partner with additional women in the worlds of music and elsewhere to share their stories of using makeup as a form of expression and reinvention.

Consumer products, the division of L'Oréal that Maybelline sits under, grew 4.3% on a like-for-like basis in Q3, lagging other segments like dermatological beauty and professional products.

#7425. Paleovalley & Wild Pastures: Customer Service Agent (US, Remote)

Location: This is a fully remote U.S.-based position. Candidates must reside and work in one of the following states due to tax and compliance requirements: AR, AZ, CA, CO, FL, GA, ID, IL, LA, MD, MO, NC, NJ, NV, NY, OH, TX, VA, WA, WI, or WY.

Compensation: This position has geographic-based pay ranges. Final compensation and geographic tier placement are determined based on the candidate's primary work location, skills, experience, and qualifications.

Tier 1 (Higher Cost Markets): $20.50 - $24.00

Tier 2 (National Markets): $19.00 - $22.00

Tier 3 (Lower Cost Markets): $17.00 - $19.50

Paleovalley and Wild Pastures were born from a simple but radical idea: real food can heal — people, animals, and the planet. When Paleovalley co-founders Autumn and Chas Smith saw how industrialized food was affecting human health and the health of the land, they refused to play along. Autumn’s own journey of healing through nutrient-dense foods lit a fire that became Paleovalley. From there, the mission grew roots, sprouted wings, and eventually expanded into Wild Pastures to bring regeneratively raised meats directly to families nationwide.

Today, Paleovalley crafts deeply nourishing snacks and supplements made with organic ingredients and honorable sourcing. Wild Pastures connects small American farmers practicing regenerative agriculture with customers who believe better food is worth fighting for. Every product, every partnership, and every decision is guided by a commitment to real ingredients, regenerative practices, and the belief that health is built from the soil up.

We’re a team of grounded doers, curious thinkers, bold builders, and warm humans who like to laugh while doing meaningful work. We're growing quickly, fueled by purpose and a shared desire to repair the food system one thoughtful choice at a time. If you feel energized by impact, drawn to mission, and ready to join a company rewriting the future of food, we’d love to meet you!

For this position, as the first point of contact for our Wild Pastures customers, this person will provide friendly, accurate, and timely support through email, live chat, and phone.

In this role, you’ll help customers navigate common questions about their accounts and orders, bringing empathy, professionalism, and attention to detail to each interaction.

You’ll work assigned shifts as part of a coordinated team schedule that helps us stay available for customers and share the workload. This role is responsible for being available during your scheduled hours and communicating promptly when your availability changes. This helps the team plan coverage, support one another, and provide a consistent customer service experience.

Paleovalley and Wild Pastures are proud to be equal opportunity employers. We make  employment decisions based on qualifications, merit, and business needs, and we do not  discriminate based on any status protected by applicable federal, state, or local law. We’re also  committed to providing reasonable accommodations throughout the hiring process. If you need  assistance or an accommodation at any point, please reach out to [email protected].

All applicants must be authorized to work in the United States. At this time, Paleovalley and  Wild Pastures are unable to sponsor or assume sponsorship of employment visas, including but  not limited to H-1B, TN, or OPT. Any offer of employment is contingent upon completion of  Form I-9 and verification of work authorization. We participate in E-Verify to confirm eligibility  to work in the U.S.

To support an efficient and consistent application review process, we may use technology assisted tools to help organize and review applications; however, these tools do not make  hiring decisions. All final hiring decisions are made by our team.

No forms. Just smart, optimized applications.

Remember every detail and next step, without a meeting bot.

#7424. Product Manager (PM) - AI x EdTech - French fluent 🇫🇷 at Paradox

It’s the chance to define a new category : The Operating System (OS) for Human Greatness Augmented with AI.

Science-backed. Design-led. AI-powered. Global.

A chance to build an AI-native Product Department

You’ll be working with the most complex data in the world : the human mind.

Deep inside you are looking for a new adventure with…

🚀Ambition around AI field : Smart teammates driven to create an iconic, international product

🔥Impact : you strive to make a real difference in the company's growth, and not be a number in a big organization

😍Mission : knowing your greatness left a mark in people's lives, through technology and behavioral science

At Paradox, we believe that greatness is trainable.

That’s why we want to empower people at scale by leveraging cutting edge technologies like AI, and crafting user experiences with outstanding aesthetics.

- Human Intelligence (coaches, peers, and mentors)

- Structured Intelligence (protocols, mental models, rituals),

- Technological Intelligence (AI personalization, feedback loops, adaptive journeys)

To create one seamless ecosystem: Paradox OS.

This is why we are looking for a PM to make Paradox a category-defining global brand loved for its product.

This is a full-time position from our beautiful offices in Dubai, Paris or Lisbon or remotely anywhere in the world.

We believe self-help is broken.Most learning platforms are used the same way : consuming generic theories, getting quick highs, ending with no real change.

At Paradox, we believe people deserve more.More than content. More than motivation.

They deserve a system that knows them, one that adapts, challenges, and grows with them.

We believe that every human learns differently, and that technology can do more than entertain or save time.It can translate insight into transformation, through AI personalization, lean experimentation, and deep psychological understanding.

Because learning shouldn’t just make you feel inspired for a day, it should change how you live, think, and create.

At Paradox, we want to re-invent how people grow.

We do not build another course or coaching app but the Operating System for Greatness.

Founded by David Laroche - a creator followed by over 2 million people and known for his interviews with icons like Tony Parker, Mike Horn, Richard Branson, and Gad Elmaleh, Paradox aims to build something that will live beyond any individual (including its founder), for decades to come.

Our ambition is to compete with players like Mindvalley (150M ARR), but with a sharper vision, grounded protocols for achievers, people who want more for their life, refusing mediocrity.

To do that, we bring together designers, tech, coaches, and filmmakers to create learning experiences that are cinematic, precise, and effective.

We move fast, aim high, and hold ourselves to the standards of Apple, Spotify, and MasterClass. No fluff, focused on meaningful impact.

While others hide their numbers, we want to let our results speak loudly for us.

Get obsessed with transforming our clients’ lives and let those transformations be our marketing.

We want to build an AI-native company, with agents in every department doing the work alongside our people.

In product, that means building a self-learning AI for product management: a system that learns from every user signal, experiment and release, and gets sharper at deciding what to build next.

For our clients, it means a self-learning AI that serves them through psychology and coaching, and gets better at helping each person change with every interaction.

You won’t just use AI tools: you’ll help design the agents, loops and feedback systems that make Paradox learn faster than anyone else.

-----------------------------------------

As Product Manager, you’ll be the architect of a new category, transforming growth into a living ecosystem powered by AI, behavioral science, and design.

You won’t just build features, you’ll craft experiences that rewire behavior.

You’re the architect bringing clarity between vision and execution, the person who turns “we should build this” into “it’s live, here’s the data and tangible impact.”

Every product cycle you lead will integrate:

Protocols : guided frameworks grounded in psychology that rewire perception, emotion, and action.

Pods : human connection, accountability, and shared rituals.

Loops : adaptive, AI-powered coaching cycles that evolve with each user.

Personalization : experiences that feel uniquely theirs, every time.

Going from a successful personal brand, to an international tech-driven iconic brand, is far from easy. It will be messy, chaotic - requiring constant iterations on MVPs and innovation in tech. Therefore to achieve this here are :

-----------------------------------------

Translate the founder’s strategic vision into a clear product strategy.

Build and maintain the roadmap that connects business goals, user outcomes, and technological innovation.

Ensure every feature supports the evolution of the Paradox ecosystem: Loops, PX Agents, Protocols, Membership.

Be obsessed with user problems : talk to users weekly, analyze patterns, and identify where transformation breaks.

Turn insights from behavioral science, coaching psychology, and learning design into actionable product hypotheses.

Design loops, feedback, and rituals that make growth tangible.

build for both the heart (inspiration) and the brain (structure).

Apply Lean Startup methodology and scientific thinking to validate ideas through MVPs, not opinions.

Design experiments and measurable success metrics before building.

Iterate on a weekly basis on new hypothesis

Define and track product success metrics (activation, retention, transformation rates).

Connect data to business KPIs, know how your product drives lifetime value, not just engagement.

We don’t track metrics just for internal dashboards, we track them also to prove transformation.

Help marketing leverage this data to fuel authentic, evidence-based storytelling — showing the world what real transformation looks like.

Create a visceral experience that translates into emotions of inspiration, awe from users.

Use a Science-backed, Cinematic, Contrarian style : Every product touchpoint should be grounded in behavioral science, expressed with cinematic beauty, and challenge conventions

-----------------------------------------

3–6 years as a Product Manager / Product Owner in EdTech, SaaS, or Consumer Tech.

Bonus: experience in AI-driven products, learning design, or personal development platforms.

You’ve shipped at least one major feature or product end-to-end, and can show measurable results.

Uses Figma comfortably, prototypes directly.

User & Impact Obsessed: You think in outcomes, not outputs, you care about transformation.

Analytical & System Thinker: You can turn a vague problem into a clear hypothesis, metrics, and roadmap.

Digital & AI savvy : you’re able to build AI Agents (if not, you demonstrate ability to learn while applying).

Cross-disciplinary Curiosity: You speak both tech and human; You can move from vision to spreadsheet to Figma

English: Fluent English, you can hold a conversation on specific details with English-speaking colleagues.

#7422. LinkedIn launches CTV ads in the UK

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The company said that its connected TV ads are 2.6 times more likely to reach B2B audiences than other CTV options.

LinkedIn announced an expansion of its connected TV ad offering, with U.K.-based businesses now also able to launch CTV campaigns.

LinkedIn’s CTV ads let advertisers activate video campaigns that will be displayed on their audience’s home TV sets via a range of display partners.

For the U.K. market, LinkedIn is partnering with LG, Paramount+, Titan OS and others.

LinkedIn advertisers will be able to launch CTV campaigns via LinkedIn Campaign Manager, with LinkedIn’s system able to present ads to “eligible viewers watching ad-supported streaming TV across participating publishers,” per the company.

LinkedIn launched CTV ads with U.S. advertisers in April 2024 as another means to tap into rising video consumption trends and expand campaign reach beyond its app.

LinkedIn also shared research highlighting the value of CTV ads in reaching relevant audiences.

According to the company, 81% of global B2B CMOs have indicated that video is helping to increase the sales cycle and drive impact. In addition, people who saw a brand’s CTV ad first cost that brand 30% less to convert into sales leads.

LinkedIn also noted that its CTV ads are 2.6 times more effective at building brand awareness among B2B audiences than other CTV options.

The expansion of LinkedIn’s CTV offering will facilitate more opportunities, helping to extend marketing pushes beyond the app.

#7421. Fetch trades views for rewards with brand-backed holiday microseries

“The Receipt” features brands like Dove, Breyer’s and Olipop across six episodes and offers consumers points for viewing that can be redeemed for gift cards.

Fetch is angling to become a more integral part of the holiday shopping experience with what it bills as the industry’s first rewarded romantic comedy. The effort leverages an in-app rewarded video format launched by Fetch in August alongside WPP Media, which helped bring the tool to market, and Unilever, which served as a launch partner. In early tests of the format, Fetch observed a 96% video completion rate, per details shared in the launch announcement.

Privately-owned Fetch operates by allowing consumers to upload receipts from everyday purchases to earn points that can be exchanged for gift cards. The company sees over 13 million receipts submitted each day and has awarded over $1 billion in points to date, per release details. Over the holidays, Fetch expects consumers to redeem over $100 million in rewards, said founder and CEO Wes Schroll in a press statement.

“The Receipt” follows the story of Chloe, played by Condor, after a meet-cute with Max, played by De La Torre — Condor’s real-life husband — results in a tote bag swap. To reconnect with Max, Chloe relies on shopping receipts found in his bag and the help of best friend Liam, played by Sedeño, a creator with nearly 1.5 million followers on TikTok. The series, created with director Michael Hoy, also features creators from Fetch’s Creator Club ambassador program.

New episodes of the series will launch every three days until Dec. 18 on Fetch’s Instagram, YouTube and TikTok channels. Each episode will feature a single brand partner, with participants including Olipop, Breyers, Dove, health food brand Kize and CPG manufacturing company Nehemiah, a close partner of Procter & Gamble. General Mills brands Betty Crocker and Blue Buffalo will be featured together in one episode. The featured brands will each be tied into a storyline in the series, pushing past traditional product placement in a move that could drive stronger brand recall among viewers.

Within the Fetch app, consumers can watch the series trailer, which launches Nov. 20, and episodes to earn points that can be redeemed for gift cards. Users can also earn points by shopping the episodes within the in-app Fetch Shop, or complete Fetch Play gaming challenges to unlock bonus content.

The global cashback apps market was valued at $6.12 billion last year and is expected to grow to nearly $12 billion by 2034 as consumers attempt to make their dollars stretch further. Average household spending over the 2026 holiday season is expected to reach $1,654, a 4% increase over the year-ago period, according to Deloitte.

The latest effort from Fetch taps into the growing trend around branded microdramas and episodic content. Others that have leveraged a similar strategy recently include L’Oréal’s Urban Decay, Marc Jacobs and JCPenney.

#7418. YouTube announces ‘Grand Theft Auto VI’ activations

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The platform will host a 24-hour global gaming livestream event on the day the video game launches and offer limited-edition digital gifts.

YouTube's looking to tap into increased creator interest around the launch of the latest Grand Theft Auto game, with the platform set to host a continuous, 24-hour launch day livestream for GTA VI that will feature a range of top gaming creators.

The global-scale gaming stream will be promoted in the app and users can tap on a custom GTA VI-themed YouTube logo on the homepage to view the stream.

The stream will offer a showcase of gaming creators as well as a look at the new game. It will also provide fans with a means to link into the broader community excitement surrounding the launch.

In addition to the streaming event, YouTube is launching limited-edition, game-themed digital gifts, “including iconic animations like Wanted Stars that pop up directly on screen during streams,” the company said.

It’s also added a “GTA: Visit Vice City” Shorts Effect, which will enable Shorts creators to depict themselves within the game world. Users will also be able to access the GTA VI soundtrack on the YouTube Music homepage throughout launch week.

YouTube also published a new Culture and Trends report exploring the rise of GTA-related content across the app.

Gaming culture has played a major role in the rise of YouTube, with gaming and gaming-related creators always featured heavily among its top channels.

The GTA series, in particular, has prompted a heap of YouTube content, with the platform claiming that videos related to the series have generated more than 900 billion views in the app.

It makes sense, then, for YouTube to give this long-awaited launch a specific focus and highlight broader community engagement in the app.

#7415. How Unilever’s social-first marketing is evolving amid reorganization

Leandro Barreto will be promoted to chief growth and marketing officer on Jan. 1, a move that’s in line with how other marketing leaders are adding responsibility for the P&L.

After seeing strong sales and volume growth last quarter, Unilever upgraded its outlook for the rest of 2026 and now expects at least 3% underlying volume growth for the full year. But the CPG giant is not standing still as it looks to build on that growth: At an investor event last week, the company announced plans to roll out a reorganized operating model in 2027 that will be centered around a “global strategy, local execution” principle.

Unilever’s operations will be split into six global units, with leadership responsible for, among other things, managing the growing convergence of content, media and commerce. In addition, Leandro Barreto, currently chief marketing officer of Unilever and its beauty and wellbeing business, will be appointed chief growth and marketing officer on Jan. 1, a move that’s in line with how other marketing leaders are adding responsibility for the P&L.

The reorganization arrives as Unilever continues to evolve into a pure-play home and personal care company following the separation of its ice cream business last year and the planned spinoff of its food business as part of a $44.8 billion deal with McCormick. The latter hit an antitrust snag in the U.K. this week that Unilever described as an “expected step” in the process.

Moving forward, Unilever plans to focus on four pillars when operating its brands: increasing the role of wellness, developing authority systems, executing flawlessly and remaining committed to a marketing philosophy it implemented last year called SASSY, which is an acronym for superior science, aesthetics, sensorials, shared by others and young spirited principles.

The “shared by others” part of SASSY includes the company’s work to shift half of digital ad spend to social-first and creator-driven marketing. Unilever reported that it now relies on 500,000 content creators, up from the previously reported 300,000 and significantly more than the 20,000 utilized before CEO Fernando Fernandez joined the company.

“We have embraced social-first marketing at a very significant speed, and this is showing in the way we are growing our key brands,” Fernandez said at Unilever’s investor event on Nov. 4. “Some of our brands have been really rejuvenated… Have we done it in all our portfolio? No. We have great examples, and we have brands in which we are starting to replicate this model.”

Currently, authority systems in a content-overloaded, artificial intelligence-powered world mediated by agentic shopping and LLMs represent a gap in Unilever’s strategy and an area where some competitors have outperformed the company.

“The algorithms have to follow a logic, and the logic they follow is geographical proximity, independent recommendation, clinical proof, patents published, ratings and review. This plays in favour of great brands with great science, with great budgets,” Fernandez explained.

AI is affecting Unilever’s business in a variety of ways, including shifting the innovation process from primarily being a physical discipline to a digital one. The company can now develop consumer signals into validated concepts in hours instead of months.

“We now can see very clearly how the signals are operating, and we can, with synthetic audiences, not only spot more than 60,000 consumer signals in a continuous way, but we also can sharpen those ideas before even real consumers see them,” Barreto said during the investor event. “The innovation also is leveraging way more data points, more than 25 million of them. That's why we can match the formulation with the concept, and we can have predictions on performance and consumer appeal.”

Unilever’s social-first marketing model involves four areas at scale: recommendation, content, influence and media. To solve for recommendation that is mediated by LLMs, Unilever said it is structuring its data to be machine-readable, working with partners like Google and optimizing by tracking more than one million prompts across more than 30 markets. In the last six months, Unilever has improved in more than 90% of the brand market sales positions that it tracks.

The company is using an AI-powered engine to meet the scale and speed of its content needs, and reports that it is producing twelve times more content than in 2024 at a 90% lower cost. In some cases, purchase intent and brand recall have increased, due in part to training AI on each brand’s DNA and core proposition. AI is also helping Unilever find, brief, vet and measure creators whose content yields ROI that is 1.5-times higher than brand-led content.

The proliferation of AI is also one of the factors reducing the lifespan of video content to four days. After that time, content on platforms like Meta, Google, TikTok, Amazon and Walmart has negative — not simply diminishing — levels of return, making marketers like Unilever rethink how it approaches content.

“The level of engagement that you provide to the platform fundamentally changes the price you pay. This is a fundamental change and makes development of content such an important capability in a company these days,” Fernandez said.

Unilever said it has dramatically reduced the cost per unit of content and improved its return on investment between 10% and 15% per year. However, that ROI is not expected to return to the company’s P&L or bottom line, but serve as a cost of doing business in a digital media environment.

“In the past, a company like Unilever could gain a significant competitive advantage by purchasing large amounts of media from TV Globo in Brazil. Today, however, media is being purchased through Meta, Google, Amazon, Walmart and TikTok, and that model no longer favors scale of purchase. Instead, it favors the quality of engagement you generate on the platform,” Fernandez said. “I believe improving our return on investment is fundamental to keeping our business model working, continuing to drive top-line growth and delivering modest margin expansion every year.”

#7413. Senior Product Builder - Hub at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

Camunda Hub is where enterprises run Camunda at scale. From Hub they manage their clusters and tenants, decide who can do what, and roll changes out across the whole organization. We’re looking for a Senior Product Builder to own the backend and architecture that let Hub manage a whole fleet of clusters. Your first focus will be the mechanism that sends access, secret, tenant and licence changes out to every connected cluster and brings deployments, health and audit records back. From there, you’ll shape Hub’s foundations: its event model, the service boundaries and data model other teams build on, and how Hub behaves when things fail or get upgraded. The platform is your product. Your users are platform administrators, Centres of Excellence and the builders across Camunda who depend on what you create. You’ll join a new two-person pod in our remote-first team. You’ll have real ownership as the person who makes the call, and you’ll build AI-first, from written specs through to production. Our customers run mission-critical processes on Camunda, so your work will matter to them every day.

Take over Hub's fleet propagation mechanism from the team building its first version, and make it work for access, secrets, tenants, licences and custom roles

Write short design documents for foundational changes, get them reviewed by the teams that depend on them, and turn them into tested, shipped code

Design how Hub behaves when things go wrong: offline clusters, partial failures, Hub outages, and clusters being adopted or detached

Build the path from clusters back to Hub, starting with deployment inventory, health, audit records and licence status

Design Hub's first event and webhook mechanism, so admins and agents can react to changes instead of polling for them

Join the pod's rotations and Hub on-call, and help other builders take on backend work through reviews, guidelines and contract tests

Experience building and running backend systems in production that other teams or customers depend on

Comfort taking a problem nobody owns yet and turning it into a design, a plan and shipped code

Product judgment for technical users: you talk to the people who use your APIs and make trade-offs on their behalf

Clear, short writing that lets a remote team review and decide asynchronously

Care for how a system behaves when parts of it fail, not only for the happy path

Designed and shipped a control plane or similar system that pushes desired state to many targets. Knows outbox or event-log patterns, idempotent apply, versioning, retries and drift reconciliation

Senior backend engineering in Java (API design, relational data models, multi-tenant SaaS services) and hands-on Helm and Kubernetes for self-managed installations

Software architecture and failure analysis: partial failure, blast radius, consistency trade-offs. Has debugged production incidents in a distributed system and is ready for on-call

Has shaped an API or platform as a product, and writes design docs other teams can build on. Gets agreement across teams without formal authority

Builds AI-first from written specifications and checks AI output as carefully as any other code

Kubernetes operators or GitOps tools, for example Argo CD

OIDC, SCIM and permission models, or secret managers and licence systems

Camunda 8 or another workflow or orchestration engine

Enough frontend skill to build a thin UI on top of a backend capability

#7412. Instagram experiments with new sports feature

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The app is reportedly testing a capability that would enable users to specify the sports that they’re interested in.

As Threads continues to add more sport-related elements, in order to tap into the value of sports engagement, and in particular, live sport discussion, Instagram is now also seemingly exploring sports engagement options, in order to expand that push.

As per the above screenshot, which was shared by app researcher Alessandro Paluzzi on X, Instagram seems to be experimenting with a new sports-related feature that would enable IG users to specify sports that they’re interested in.

The sports listing would seemingly enable users to select from a list of top sports and customize the types of updates they want to see from each league.

Instagram would then use that info to highlight relevant, real-time sports updates and showcase more sports content to interested users.

Sports content may not immediately stand out as a key focus on IG, but sports discussion overall drives significant engagement across Meta’s apps.

In September 2025, Meta published a report on rising sports engagement across its platforms. That study noted that Meta apps are among the primary online destinations for sports engagement.

Doubling down on that, then, could be a valuable focus.

Sports remains the most discussed topic on X and that platform is also a key real-time engagement driver. That’s important for an app like Threads, which is looking to become a bigger focus for event-related discussion.

But Instagram is also a key platform for sports engagement, with Boardroom recently reporting that some of the most followed people in the app are sports stars.

Earlier this year, Meta also reported that interest in women’s sport is rising in its apps, with 128 million people combined now following the top 10 women athletes on Instagram.

That could present a significant opportunity for Instagram to tap into rising sports interest, and this new feature could be another means to drive more sports engagement.

Instagram did not immediately respond to Social Media Today’s request for comment.

#7408. Meta’s Edits app gets speed curves and auto audio balancing

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

New features enable creators to change the playback speed of their videos and ensure better audio experiences.

Meta announced its latest feature updates for the Edits video editing app, including speed curves for variable playback speed, audio auto-adjust and text animation duration controls.

First, Edits users can now implement speed curves in their clips, which implements controlled playback speeds for more impact at certain points.

As shown in the first image above, creators now have a range of speed curve options to choose from, which will increase and/or decrease the video playback speed during a clip.

Creators can also create their own speed curves.

The second screenshot above shows the updated audio balancing tools, which will enable creators to automatically balance audio levels across video elements.

Edits also wants to show creators more relevant content matches in its Inspiration tab, so users can now choose what topics they want to see more or less of in this element.

The feature is somewhat similar to Meta’s Your Algorithm tool, which enables users to highlight the topics they’re interested or not interested in, in order to refine their news feed.

This is more specifically focused on the clips users are shown on Edits as inspiration for their own content, but it provides a similar manual management capacity.

The second image above also shows new text animation duration controls, which will enable creators to define how long the text in their video is active for. That will give creators the capacity to dictate how fast text appears and disappears, and how fast the animation loops, among other considerations.

Meta has continued to roll out updates for Edits almost every other week, with an array of video creation features that have made it an increasingly valuable tool.

And with Edits also now coming to desktop PCs, that could expand its value once again, with all of these elements displayed on a much larger screen.

#7406. Sociable: Meta’s AI ads included unapproved discounts

Recent reports found that the artificial intelligence-powered ad tools posted discount offers to customers even with promotions turned off.

“Sociable” is the latest commentary on important social media developments and trends from industry expert Andrew Hutchinson of Social Media Today.

As artificial intelligence tools become more empowered, new parameters need to be established to limit their capacity when it comes to undertaking certain tasks.

There have already been reports of an AI tool giving out a user’s home address, generating hundreds of videos at a users’ expense and conducting questionable crypto trades.

Automated errors can also extend to buying digital ads. PPC Land reported last week that Meta’s AI-powered ad tools took discounts from a store’s website and displayed them on the business’s Facebook and Instagram ads, “even when the shop had turned promotions off on the ad it copied.”

The discovery was made by digital marketing advisor Bram Van der Hallen, who shared a LinkedIn post outlining the discovery.

As per Van der Hallen: “Meta may now automatically detect offers from your website and use them in your Meta ads. Even when you have turned off this option for your original ad. When the option to display offers detected from your website and ads is enabled in Commerce Manager, Meta may then automatically display these offers in your ads.”

Van der Hallen said the issue can be controlled by updating Commerce Manager settings to stop Meta from using detected offers in other campaigns.

But this adds another element to consider, and PPC Land noted that it could cause issues for advertisers: “This matters for any business advertising on Meta with more than one offer live, because a discount meant for one campaign or one audience could show up somewhere else.”

Meta recently outlined how its expanded processing capacity is helping generate more effective ad campaigns. The company said that improvements to its ads ranking system drove a more than 8% increase in ad clicks and a 15.7% uplift in conversions on Facebook in Q2 2026.

However, as more systems are guided by AI, there will be errors because AI systems seek out efficiencies and optimizations wherever they can, which could cause issues.

Advertisers need to be aware of these potential issues, which could have a big impact on campaign approaches.

#7401. Welch’s holiday microdrama embraces Y2K nostalgia as Gen Z become parents

The five-episode series features the actress who voiced Kim in “Kim Possible” and a member of The Cheetah Girls group.

Welch’s is jumping on the microdrama trend as consumers head into the holiday season. “Before the Toast” features many of the trappings typically associated with the format — like absurdly high stakes and plenty of drama — and a nostalgia-fueled cast to promote the brand’s line of sparkling juices during a time of year when demand is usually high.

Joining Romano in the series is her husband, Brendan Rooney, Sabrina Bryan of The Cheetah Girls and Heather Morris, from “Glee.” Storylines largely revolve around navigating holiday season chaos with young children and creating new memories.

The actors chosen for the campaign, in addition to its strategic placement around family-friend holiday programming, may help the brand reach families with younger children. The demand for sparkling juices has gone up as consumers look for alternatives to soda and alcoholic beverages. This season also sees the introduction of Welch’s Sparkling Low Sugar Grape, which is available at Walmart.

The cast may also be nostalgic for Gen Z parents who grew up on Disney Channel shows, many of whom are sharing the media with their own children.

As part of the campaign, the brand teamed up with Instacart to give away full-size bottles of its products in the lead-up to Thanksgiving and Christmas. Shoppers are able to claim the offer via the product card on the Instacart app.

Microdramas are being embraced by brands looking to reach consumers. The format has proven popular with Gen Z, who have grown used to watching short, vertical videos on their phones. The scale of efforts have varied, from just a few episodes to P&G’s 50-part series for Native. Welch’s isn’t the first beverage brand to jump on the microdrama bandwagon. In May, Dr Pepper promoted the return of its Creamy Coconut product with a four-episode series in which a young woman has a fleeting love affair with a can of the dirty soda-inspired flavor.

Welch’s, which operates as an agricultural cooperative and does not release earnings statements, replaced its CMO position with a chief brand and innovation officer in October of last year. Andrew Hartshorn, who previously held roles at PepsiCo and Danone, assumed the role. Shortly after he arrived, the brand introduced a new kid mascot, marking the first time in 25 years that Welch’s creative starred a child character.

#7397. AI-derived audience insights hurt results: What the numbers say

The majority of marketing leaders have encountered inaccurate or misleading AI findings, according to GWI research.

From eliminating mundane tasks to the creation of full-fledged campaigns, artificial intelligence has become a central focus for marketers as departments feel the effects of budget constraints. Nearly 90% of senior industry professionals use AI daily, according to a survey by GWI. However, the technology isn’t without its drawbacks, with 80% of respondents saying they frequently encounter inaccurate or misleading AI findings.

“Broadly, everyone is using AI, everyone feels very confident and good about it, but everyone also has very strong concerns and suspicions, and remarkably high numbers are seeing negative impact or less than good outputs. But we're carrying on using it regardless,” said Jason Mander, chief insight officer at GWI.

“Artifical Confidence: The AI Trust Gap,” includes responses from 1,883 senior insights, agency and marketing professionals based in the U.K., U.S. and Singapore collected in the third quarter of 2026. All respondents worked at companies with over 500 employees or $26.5 million (20 million pounds) in annual turnover in areas such as media, agencies, gaming, retail and others.

While AI is known to produce incorrect information, only 36% of survey respondents say they verify AI insights “most of the time.” Not only can AI insights be incorrect, they are often out-of-date, with 72% agreeing that the technology can make something seem more relevant than it actually is. Overconfidence has consequences: 63% of respondents say they have accidentally used insights they later realized were out of date and 68% think their colleagues are doing the same thing without noticing.

“We've got somewhat lost in the buzz and the excitement, and haven't been as rigorous as we otherwise would be,” said Mander. “I think part of that is a fear of not keeping up. This adoption has been so rapid and so public and so well known that there's a pressure on professionals that if you're not using AI, you feel like you're behind the curve and you might be losing out to competitors.”

AI models can over rely on stereotypes when it comes to audience profiles and 76% industry leaders think the tech can reinforce outdated assumptions. Nearly 70% of respondents have seen the technology fail to accurately reflect their organization’s target audience in profiles. Despite this, 81% said they feel confident in using AI for audience profiles and segmentation, and 84% believe the technology can accurately explain human behavior, per the report.

The technology is also seen as largely unable to decipher consumers' motivations, with 71% of respondents saying AI can tell you “what” but not “why.” Eighty percent said that fully understanding motivations still requires human involvement and 75% believe AI will never replace direct human research. However, over 90% of organizations are building or already using AI tools that depend on external data.

While AI has been seen as a potential solution to budgetary constraints, not thoroughly checking its outputs can have real effects on business functioning. Over half of senior decision-makers have made a significant business decision based on AI insights that turned out to be incorrect or misleading, per the report.

Feeling the negative impacts of inaccurate AI insights is common, with 82% of marketing leaders feeling at least one adverse outcome, the most common being internal rework (38%), followed by delayed decision making (36%) and customer complaints (34%).

Putting too much trust in AI carries its own set of risks and 21% of survey respondents say they have seen weaker campaign performance due to mistakes made by the technology. Other observed adverse outcomes include financial loss (27%) and reputational damage (26%).

“I think we've got a little bit lost in all of the benefits because AI…allows you to become so much more productive, save so much time, increase all of your efficiencies. You perhaps haven't scrutinized some of the downsides,” said Mander. “But it also suggests that, generally speaking, companies haven't put the type of rigorous frameworks in place that you would expect with a new type of technology.”

Correction: A previous version of this article misstated the name of the report. It has been updated with the correct name, “Artificial Confidence: The AI Trust Gap.”
